The slope of a line is determined by the tangent of its angle with the positive x-axis. For an angle of 120°, tan(120°) equals -√3 because it's in the second quadrant where tangent values are negative.

**Step-by-step explanation:**
1. Recognize that the slope (m) is given by m = tan(theta), where theta is the angle with the positive x-axis.
2. Convert 120 degrees to a reference angle within the first quadrant, which is 60 degrees (since 180° - 120° = 60°).
3. Use the identity that tan(180° - theta) = -tan(theta), so tan(120°) = -tan(60°).
4. Recall that tan(60°) is √3.
5. Therefore, m = -√3.

The area of the quadrilateral with vertices at (-6,7), (2,9), (7,-3), and (-2,0) is calculated using the shoelace formula. The calculation involves multiplying coordinates in a specific order and taking half the absolute difference between two sums.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **List the Coordinates:** Arrange the vertices in order.
   - A(-6,7), B(2,9), C(7,-3), D(-2,0)

2. **Apply Shoelace Formula:**
   - Sum of products of x_i and y_{i+1}:
     (-6)*9 + 2*(-3) + 7*0 + (-2)*7 = -54 -6 +0 -14 = -74
   - Sum of products of y_i and x_{i+1}:
     7*2 + 9*7 + (-3)*(-2) +0*(-6) = 14 +63 +6 +0 =83

3. **Compute Area:**
   |Sum1 - Sum2| / 2 = |-74 -83| / 2 = 157/2 = 78.5

**Answer:** The area is \boxed{\dfrac{157}{2}} square units.

To find a point on the **x-axis** that is equidistant from the points \((9, 8)\) and \((5, 6)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand the Problem
We need to determine a point \((x, 0)\) on the x-axis such that its distance from both given points is equal.

### Step 2: Set Up the Distance Equations
The squared distances between \((x, 0)\) and each of the given points are:

\[
\text{Distance}^2 \text{ to } (9, 8): (x - 9)^2 + (0 - 8)^2 = (x - 9)^2 + 64
\]

\[
\text{Distance}^2 \text{ to } (5, 6): (x - 5)^2 + (0 - 6)^2 = (x - 5)^2 + 36
\]

### Step 3: Set the Distances Equal and Solve for \(x\)
Since the distances are equal:

\[
(x - 9)^2 + 64 = (x - 5)^2 + 36
\]

Expanding both sides:

\[
x^2 - 18x + 81 + 64 = x^2 - 10x + 25 + 36
\]

Simplify the equation:

\[
-18x + 145 = -10x + 61
\]

Bring all terms to one side:

\[
-8x + 84 = 0
\]

Solve for \(x\):

\[
x = \frac{84}{8} = 10.5
\]

### Final Answer
The point on the x-axis equidistant from both given points is at \(x = 10.5\).

To find the equation of the line that passes through the point (0, 5) and has an x-intercept at -2, we can use the two-point method. 

1. Identify the given points: (0, 5) is on the y-axis, and (-2, 0) is the x-intercept.
2. Calculate the slope (m) using these two points:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{0 - 5}{-2 - 0} = \frac{-5}{-2} = \frac{5}{2}
   \]
3. Use the slope-intercept form of the equation, knowing that the y-intercept is 5:
   \[
   y = \frac{5}{2}x + 5
   \]
4. Convert this to standard form (Ax + By = C):
   \[
   y = \frac{5}{2}x + 5 \\
   Multiply both sides by 2: \\
   2y = 5x + 10 \\
   Rearrange: \\
   -5x + 2y = 10 \\
   Multiply both sides by -1 to match the standard form: \\
   5x - 2y = -10
   \]

Thus, the equation of the line is \boxed{5x - 2y = -10}.

I need to find the equation of a line that passes through two given points, (3, 1) and (5, 7).

To find the equation of the line passing through the points \((3, 1)\) and \((5, 7)\), follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the slope (m):**

   The formula for the slope between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is:
   
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   
   Plugging in the given points:
   
   \[
   m = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3
   \]

2. **Use the point-slope form to find the equation:**

   The point-slope form of a line is:
   
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   
   Using one of the points, for example, \((3, 1)\):
   
   \[
   y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
   \]

3. **Simplify to slope-intercept form:**

   Expanding and simplifying:
   
   \[
   y - 1 = 3x - 9
   \]
   
   Adding 1 to both sides:
   
   \[
   y = 3x - 8
   \]

**Final Answer:** The equation of the line is \(\boxed{y = 3x - 8}\).

**Final Answer**
The equation of the line is \boxed{\dfrac{4x}{h} + \dfrac{y}{k} = 5}.
</think>

Given that a line intersects the x-axis at point \( (a, 0) \) and the y-axis at point \( (0, b) \), we need to find the equation of the line such that it divides these intercepts in the ratio 1:4 from the origin.

1. The point \( (h, k) \) divides the segment joining the intercepts \( (a, 0) \) and \( (0, b) \) internally in the ratio 1:4.
2. Using the section formula for internal division, the coordinates of point \( P(h, k) \) are given by:
   \[
   h = \frac{4a}{5} \quad \text{and} \quad k = \frac{b}{5}
   \]
3. Solving for \( a \) and \( b \):
   \[
   a = \frac{5h}{4} \quad \text{and} \quad b = 5k
   \]
4. The equation of the line passing through these intercepts is:
   \[
   \frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1
   \]
5. Substituting \( a = \frac{5h}{4} \) and \( b = 5k \):
   \[
   \frac{x}{\frac{5h}{4}} + \frac{y}{5k} = 1
   \]
6. Simplifying this equation:
   \[
   \frac{4x}{5h} + \frac{y}{5k} = 1
   \]
7. Multiplying both sides by 5 to clear the denominators:
   \[
   \frac{4x}{h} + \frac{y}{k} = 5
   \]

Thus, the equation of the line is \boxed{\dfrac{4x}{h} + \dfrac{y}{k} = 5}.

Setting up the equation: 117 = 0.075*Q + 33.

Subtracting 33 from both sides: 84 = 0.075*Q → Q = 84 / 0.075.

Okay, so I have this problem where I need to find the equation of a line that passes through the point (4, 4) and has intercepts whose sum is 13. Hmm, let me think about how to approach this step by step.

First off, I remember that the general form of a line's equation when considering its x-intercept (a) and y-intercept (b) is given by (x/a) + (y/b) = 1. This is called the intercept form of a line. So, if the sum of the intercepts is 13, that means a + b = 13.

But wait, I don't know which one is which. The problem doesn't specify whether it's the x-intercept or y-intercept, so both possibilities need to be considered. That might lead to two different equations unless they result in the same line somehow.

Given that the point (4, 4) lies on the line, I can substitute x = 4 and y = 4 into the intercept equation: (4/a) + (4/b) = 1.

So now, I have two equations:
1. a + b = 13
2. 4/a + 4/b = 1

I need to solve this system of equations for a and b. Let's see how to do that.

From the first equation, I can express one variable in terms of the other. Let's say b = 13 - a. Then substitute this into the second equation:

Now, converting this to standard form (SSS). The standard form is usually written as Ax + By = C, where A, B, C are integers and A is positive.

Starting from y = -0.5x + 2

Multiply both sides by 2 to eliminate the fraction:

2y = -x + 4

Bring x to the left side:

x + 2y = 4

So standard form (SSS) is x + 2y = 4.

Now, general form (GF). The general form is usually written as Ax + By + C = 0. So from the standard form, subtract 4 from both sides:

x + 2y - 4 = 0

So GF is x + 2y - 4 = 0.

Next, equation of line QR: points Q(-2,3) and R(4,5).

Calculate slope (m):

m = (5 - 3)/(4 - (-2))=2/6≈1/3 ≈0.333...

So m=1/3

Using point-slope with Q(-2,3):

y - 3 = (1/3)(x + 2)

Simplify to slope-intercept:

y - 3 = (1/3)x + (2/3)

Add 3 to both sides. Since 3 is 9/3,

y = (1/3)x + (2/3) + (9/3)= (1/3)x + 11/3

So SI: y = (1/3)x + 11/3

Convert to standard form:

Start with y = (1/3)x + 11/3

Multiply both sides by 3:

3y = x + 11

Bring x to left:

-x + 3y = 11

But we want A positive, so multiply both sides by -1:

x - 3y = -11

So SSS: x - 3y = -11.

General form (GF) is same as standard form rearranged with zero on the right. So from x -3y = -11,

Bring all terms to left:

x - 3y + 11 = 0

Wait, let me check.

If standard form is Ax + By = C,

then GF would be Ax + By - C =0. So from x -3y =-11,

Add 11 to both sides: x -3y +11=0

So yes, GF is x -3y +11=0.

Lastly, equation of line PR: points P(2,1) and R(4,5).

Calculate slope (m):

m=(5-1)/(4-2)=4/2=2.

Slope m=2.

Using point P(2,1):

y - 1 = 2(x -2)

Simplify to SI:

y -1 = 2x -4

Add 1: y=2x -3

So SI is y=2x-3.

Start with y=2x -3,

To find the equation of the lines forming the sides of the triangle with vertices at P(2,1), Q(-2,3), and R(4,5):

### Side PQ:
- **Standard Form (SSS):** \( x + 2y = 4 \)
- **Slope-Intercept Form (SI):** \( y = -\frac{1}{2}x + 2 \)
- **General Form (GF):** \( x + 2y - 4 = 0 \)

### Side QR:
- **Standard Form (SSS):** \( x - 3y = -11 \)
- **Slope-Intercept Form (SI):** \( y = \frac{1}{3}x + \frac{11}{3} \)
- **General Form (GF):** \( x - 3y + 11 = 0 \)

### Side PR:
- **Standard Form (SSS):** \( 2x - y = 3 \)
- **Slope-Intercept Form (SI):** \( y = 2x - 3 \)
- **General Form (GF):** \( 2x - y - 3 = 0 \)

These equations represent the three sides of the triangle formed by points P, Q, and R. Each form provides a different representation of the same line:

- **Standard Form** is useful for solving systems of equations.
- **Slope-Intercept Form** clearly shows the slope and y-intercept.
- **General Form** is often used in more advanced algebraic manipulations.

If you need further details or another type of equation, let me know!

The given equations are:
3x - 4y + 12 = 0
3x - 4y + 7 = 0

These are parallel lines since they have the same coefficients for x and y.

To find the distance (d) between them, use the formula:

d = |c₁ - c₂| / √(a² + b²)

Where:
- a = 3
- b = -4
- c₁ = 12
- c₂ = 7

Plugging in these values:

d = |12 - 7| / √(3² + (-4)²)
= 5 / √(9 + 16)
= 5 / √25
= 5 / 5
= 1

So, the distance between the two lines is 1 unit.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the given equations:** Both are linear equations in standard form (ax + by + c = 0).
   
2. **Confirm they are parallel:** Since both have coefficients a=3 and b=-4, they are indeed parallel lines.

3. **Apply the distance formula for parallel lines:**
   - The numerator is |c₁ - c₂| which is |12 - 7| = 5.
   - The denominator is √(a² + b²) where a=3 and b=-4, so √(9 + 16) = √25 = 5.

4. **Calculate the distance:** Divide the numerator by the denominator: 5/5 = 1.

**Final Answer:**
The distance between the two parallel lines is 1 unit.

The user provided an incomplete mathematical problem with unclear components and context. It seems they intended to ask about finding the maximum distance from a specific point (2,4) to lines described by y = mx + c, but without additional constraints on m and c, the maximum distance is unbounded.

Step-by-step explanation:

1. The standard formula for the distance D from a point (x₀,y₀) to the line ax + by + c = 0 is |ax₀ + by₀ + c| / sqrt(a² + b²).

2. Rewriting y = mx + c as mx - y + c = 0, so a = m, b = -1, and the constant term is c.

3. Substituting (2,4) into the formula: D = |m*2 - 1*4 + c| / sqrt(m² + (-1)^2) => D = |2m + c - 4| / sqrt(m² +1).

4. To find maximum D without constraints on m and c, it's unbounded because as either m or c approaches infinity, the numerator can become infinitely large.

5. Therefore, unless there are additional conditions (like lines passing through a specific point), the maximum distance is not achievable with finite values of m and c.

Answer: Since no additional constraints are provided, the maximum distance cannot be determined with finite m and c; it's unbounded. If more information is available about m or c, such as them lying on another line or curve, further analysis could provide a specific solution.

The foot of the perpendicular from the point (1, 5) to the line 3x - 4y - 16 = 0 is found by solving the system of equations derived from the given line and its perpendicular counterpart.

Step-by-step explanation:

1. **Find the slope of the given line**:
   The equation is 3x - 4y = 16.
   Rearranged, it becomes y = (3/4)x - 4.
   So, the slope \( m_1 \) is 3/4.

2. **Determine the slope of the perpendicular line**:
   The slope of a line perpendicular to another with slope \( m_1 \) is \( m_2 = -1/m_1 \).
   Thus, \( m_2 = -4/3 \).

3. **Write the equation of the perpendicular line passing through (1, 5)**:
   Using point-slope form: \( y - 5 = (-4/3)(x - 1) \).

4. **Convert to standard form**:
   Multiply both sides by 3: 3(y - 5) = -4(x - 1)
   Simplify: 3y - 15 = -4x + 4
   Bring all terms to one side: 4x + 3y = 19.

5. **Solve the system of equations**:
   \[
   \begin{cases}
   3x - 4y = 16 \\
   4x + 3y = 19
   \end{cases}
   \]
   Multiply the first equation by 3 and the second by 4 to eliminate \( y \):
   \[
   \begin{cases}
   9x - 12y = 48 \\
   16x + 12y = 76
   \end{cases}
   \]
   Add both equations: 25x = 124 → x = 124/25.

6. **Substitute \( x \) back into one equation to find \( y \)**:
   Using 4x + 3y = 19:
   4*(124/25) + 3y = 19
   Simplify: (496/25) + 3y = 475/25 → 3y = (-21)/25 → y = -7/25.

**Final Answer**: {Foot of the perpendicular at (x, y): "124/25, -7/25"}

The equation of the perpendicular bisector can be found by determining the midpoint and using the negative reciprocal slope of the original line segment.

1. **Find the Midpoint**:  
   The midpoint M between (5,6) and (1,4) is calculated as:  
   \[
   M = \left( \frac{5 + 1}{2}, \frac{6 + 4}{2} \right) = (3, 5)
   \]

2. **Calculate the Slope of the Original Line**:  
   The slope \( m \) between (5,6) and (1,4) is:  
   \[
   m = \frac{4 - 6}{1 - 5} = \frac{-2}{-4} = \frac{1}{2}
   \]

3. **Determine the Perpendicular Slope**:  
   The slope of the perpendicular bisector is the negative reciprocal:  
   \[
   m_{\text{perp}} = -2
   \]

4. **Equation of the Perpendicular Bisector**:  
   Using point-slope form with midpoint (3,5) and slope -2:  
   \[
   y - 5 = -2(x - 3)
   \]  
   Simplifying to standard form:  
   \[
   y = -2x + 11
   \]

To solve the system of trigonometric equations:

1. \( \sin x \cos x = 0 \)
2. \( \sin x + \cos x = 1 \)

We analyze each equation step by step.

From the first equation, \( \sin x \cos x = 0 \), we have two cases:
- Case 1: \( \sin x = 0 \) which implies \( x = k\pi \) for integer \( k \).
- Case 2: \( \cos x = 0 \) which implies \( x = \frac{\pi}{2} + m\pi \) for integer \( m \).

Next, we substitute these results into the second equation \( \sin x + \cos x = 1 \):

**Case 1: \( \sin x = 0 \)**
- Substituting \( \sin x = 0 \) into \( \sin x + \cos x = 1 \) gives \( 0 + (-1)^k = 1 \).
- This implies \( (-1)^k = 1 \), so \( k \) must be even. Therefore, \( x = 2n\pi \) for integer \( n \).

**Case 2: \( \cos x = 0 \)**
- Substituting \( \cos x = 0 \) into \( \sin x + \cos x = 1 \) gives \( (-1)^m + 0 = 1 \).
- This implies \( (-1)^m = 1 \), so \( m \) must be even. Therefore, \( x = \frac{\pi}{2} + 2p\pi \) for integer \( p \).

Thus, the solutions within the interval \([0, 2\pi)\) are:
- \( x = 0 \)
- \( x = \frac{\pi}{2} \)

Therefore, the angles that satisfy both equations are:

\[
\boxed{0} \quad \text{and} \quad \boxed{\dfrac{\pi}{2}}
\]

The equation of the line that is perpendicular to X - 7y + 5 = 0 and passes through (5, 0) is determined by finding the negative reciprocal of the original slope. 

1. Rewrite the given equation in slope-intercept form:  
   \( y = \frac{1}{7}x + \frac{5}{7} \). The slope (\( m \)) is \( \frac{1}{7} \).

2. Calculate the negative reciprocal for the perpendicular slope:  
   Perpendicular slope (\( m_{\perp} \)) is \( -7 \).

3. Use point-slope form with (5, 0):  
   \( y - 0 = -7(x - 5) \)

4. Simplify to standard form:  
   \( 7x + y = 35 \)

I need to find the equation of a line parallel to the given line \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) that passes through the point \((-4, 5)\).

First, I'll rewrite the original line in slope-intercept form (\(y = mx + b\)) to identify its slope.

Starting with:
\(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\)

Subtract \(5x\) and 4 from both sides:
\(-6y = -5x - 4\)

Divide by \(-6\):
\(y = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{2}{3}\)

So, the slope (\(m\)) of the original line is \(\frac{5}{6}\). Since parallel lines have the same slope, the new line will also have a slope of \(\frac{5}{6}\).

Next, I'll use the point-slope form to find the equation of the new line. The formula is:
\(y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)\)

Substituting \(m = \frac{5}{6}\), and the point \((-4, 5)\):
\(y - 5 = \frac{5}{6}(x + 4)\)

Distribute the slope on the right side:
\(y - 5 = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{20}{6}\)
Simplify \(\frac{20}{6}\) to \(\frac{10}{3}\):
\(y - 5 = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{10}{3}\)

Finally, add 5 to both sides to get the equation in slope-intercept form:
\(y = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{25}{6}\)
</think>

To find the equation of a line parallel to \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) that passes through the point \((-4, 5)\), follow these steps:

1. **Find the slope of the original line**:
   Convert the given line into slope-intercept form (\(y = mx + b\)):
   \[
   5x - 6y + 4 = 0 \\
   -6y = -5x - 4 \\
   y = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{2}{3}
   \]
   The slope \(m\) is \(\frac{5}{6}\).

2. **Use the point-slope form**:
   Since parallel lines have the same slope, use \(m = \frac{5}{6}\) and the point \((-4, 5)\):
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1) \\
   y - 5 = \frac{5}{6}(x + 4)
   \]

3. **Simplify to slope-intercept form**:
   \[
   y - 5 = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{20}{6} \\
   y = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{10}{3} + 5 \\
   y = \frac{5}{6}x + \frac{25}{6}
   \]

**Final Answer:**

The equation of the parallel line is:
\[
y = \boxed{\frac{5}{6}x + \frac{25}{6}}
\]

To solve the system of linear equations involving variables \( x \) and \( y \), we can use either substitution or elimination. Here's a step-by-step approach assuming two equations:

1. **Identify the Equations**: Ensure you have two distinct linear equations with variables \( x \) and \( y \).

2. **Choose a Method**:
   - **Substitution**: Solve one equation for one variable (e.g., solve Equation 1 for \( x \)) and substitute into the other equation.
   - **Elimination**: Manipulate the equations to eliminate one variable by adding or subtracting them.

3. **Solve for One Variable**: After substitution or elimination, you'll have an equation with a single variable. Solve this to find its value.

4. **Back-Substitute**: Plug the found value back into one of the original equations to solve for the remaining variable.

5. **Verify the Solution**: Substitute both values into the original equations to ensure they satisfy all given equations.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

   The original equation is \( \frac{x}{3} + \frac{y}{4} = 1 \). Multiply both sides by 12 to eliminate denominators:
   \( 4x + 3y - 12 = 0 \).

2. **Point on the x-axis:**
   Any point on the x-axis has coordinates (a, 0).

3. **Distance formula from a point to a line:**
   The distance \( D \) from a point \( (x_0, y_0) \) to the line \( Ax + By + C = 0 \) is given by:
   \( D = \frac{|Ax_0 + By_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}} \).

4. **Apply the formula:**
   Substitute \( (a, 0) \), \( A=4 \), \( B=3 \), and \( C=-12 \):
   \( D = \frac{|4a - 12|}{5} \).

5. **Set distance equal to 4 units:**
   \( \frac{|4a - 12|}{5} = 4 \).

6. **Solve for \( a \):**
   Multiply both sides by 5:
   \( |4a - 12| = 20 \).

7. **Consider the absolute value cases:**
   \( 4a - 12 = 20 \) leads to \( a = 8 \).
   \( 4a - 12 = -20 \) leads to \( a = -2 \).

8. **Final coordinates:**
   The points are (8, 0) and (-2, 0).

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

   \[
   6x + 4y = 24
   \]

   \[
   \frac{6x}{24} + \frac{4y}{24} = \frac{24}{24}

   Simplifying:
   \[
   \frac{x}{4} + \frac{y}{6} = 1

The distance from the point (1, 2) to the line \(4x + 7y + 5 = 0\) can be calculated using the formula for the distance from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\):

\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Plugging in the values:
- \(A = 4\), \(B = 7\), \(C = 5\)
- \(x_0 = 1\), \(y_0 = 2\)

Calculate the numerator:
\[
|4 \times 1 + 7 \times 2 + 5| = |4 + 14 + 5| = |23| = 23
\]

Calculate the denominator:
\[
\sqrt{4^2 + 7^2} = \sqrt{16 + 49} = \sqrt{65}
\]

So, the distance is:
\[
\frac{23}{\sqrt{65}}
\]
This can also be rationalized as:
\[
\frac{23\sqrt{65}}{65}
\]

Final Answer: {Distance: 23/sqrt(65)}

I need to calculate the distance from point P(4,1) to the line 4x - y = 0. 

First, I'll identify the formula for the distance from a point (x₀,y₀) to a line ax + by + c = 0, which is |ax₀ + by₀ + c| / sqrt(a² + b²). 

The given line equation can be rewritten as 4x - y + 0 = 0. So, a=4, b=-1, and c=0.

Plugging in the coordinates of point P(4,1), I substitute x₀=4 and y₀=1 into the formula: |4*4 + (-1)*1 + 0| / sqrt(4² + (-1)²).

Calculating the numerator: 4*4 = 16, -1*1 = -1. Adding these gives 15.

For the denominator: 4 squared is 16, and -1 squared is 1. Their sum is 17, so sqrt(17).

Therefore, the distance is 15 divided by sqrt(17), which can also be written as (15√17)/17 for rationalized form.
</think>

Then, calculate the change in x by subtracting the second x-coordinate from the first:

Δx = -2 - 6 = -8.

Finally, determine the slope (m) by dividing Δy by Δx:

m = 12 / (-8) = -3/2.

First, I'll recall the formula for calculating the slope between two points. The slope \( m \) is equal to the change in y divided by the change in x, which can be written as:

\[ m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} \]

Next, I'll assign the coordinates of the given points to this formula. Here, \( (x_1, y_1) = (5, 0) \) and \( (x_2, y_2) = (9, 0) \).

Plugging these values into the formula gives:

\[ m = \frac{0 - 0}{9 - 5} = \frac{0}{4} = 0 \]

Since both points have the same y-coordinate, the line is horizontal, and therefore, the slope of the line is zero.

The problem involves finding the slope of a second line given that it forms an angle of π/4 with another line whose slope is known as 1/4.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Understand the Formula for Angle Between Two Lines:**
   The formula to find the tangent of the angle θ between two lines with slopes \( m_1 \) and \( m_2 \) is:
   \[
   \tan(\theta) = \left| \frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 \cdot m_2} \right|
   \]
   
2. **Plug in the Given Values:**
   Here, θ = π/4, so \( \tan(\pi/4) = 1 \). Also, one slope is \( m_1 = 1/4 \), and we need to find \( m_2 \).

3. **Set Up the Equation:**
   Substituting into the formula:
   \[
   1 = \left| \frac{m_2 - (1/4)}{1 + (1/4) \cdot m_2} \right|
   \]
   
4. **Remove Absolute Value by Considering Two Cases:**

   - **Case 1:** \( \frac{m_2 - 1/4}{1 + (1/4)m_2} = 1 \)
     - Multiply both sides by denominator:
       \[
       m_2 - \frac{1}{4} = 1 + \frac{1}{4}m_2
       \]
     - Rearranging terms:
       \[
       m_2 - \frac{1}{4}m_2 = 1 + \frac{1}{4}
       \]
       \[
       \frac{3}{4}m_2 = \frac{5}{4}
       \]
       \[
       m_2 = \frac{5}{3}
       \]

   - **Case 2:** \( \frac{m_2 - 1/4}{1 + (1/4)m_2} = -1 \)
     - Multiply both sides by denominator:
       \[
       m_2 - \frac{1}{4} = -1 - \frac{1}{4}m_2
       \]
     - Rearranging terms:
       \[
       m_2 + \frac{1}{4}m_2 = -1 + \frac{1}{4}
       \]
       \[
       \frac{5}{4}m_2 = -\frac{3}{4}
       \]
       \[
       m_2 = -\frac{3}{5}
       \]

**Final Answer:**
The possible slopes of the other line are \( \boxed{\frac{5}{3}} \) and \( \boxed{-\frac{3}{5}} \).

12 = -3(x - 10)

Distribute the -3 on the right side:

The slope of the line through (0,8) and (6,10) is calculated as follows:

Slope₁ = (10 - 8)/(6 - 0) = 2/6 = 1/3.

Since the lines are perpendicular, the slope of the second line (slope₂) must be the negative reciprocal of slope₁. Therefore,

slope₂ = -1/(1/3) = -3.

Using the points (10,14) and (x,26), set up the equation for slope₂:

slope₂ = (26 - 14)/(x - 10) = -3  
12/(x - 10) = -3  
Multiply both sides by (x - 10):  
12 = -3(x - 10)  
12 = -3x + 30  
-3x = 12 - 30  
-3x = -18  
x = (-18)/(-3)  
x = 6.

Area = (1/2) * |sum over i (x_i * y_{i+1} - x_{i+1} * y_i)|

So let's compute each term step by step.

First pair: A to B
x_A * y_B = (-6)*9 = -54
x_B * y_A = 2*7 = 14
Difference: -54 -14 = -68

Second pair: B to C
x_B * y_C = 2*(-3) = -6
x_C * y_B = 7*9 = 63
Difference: -6 -63 = -69

Third pair: C to D
x_C * y_D =7*0=0
x_D * y_C = (-2)*(-3)=6
Difference:0-6=-6

Fourth pair: D to A
x_D * y_A = (-2)*7 = -14
x_A * y_D = (-6)*0=0
Difference:-14 -0 = -14

Now sum all these differences:
-68 + (-69) + (-6) + (-14) = let's calculate step by step.

First, -68 + (-69) is -137. Then, adding (-6): total is -143. Adding (-14) gives -157.

Take the absolute value: | -157 | = 157

Now multiply by 1/2: (1/2)*157 = 78.5

Wait a second, that seems right? But let me double-check my calculations to avoid any mistakes.

Let me recount each step carefully:

For A-B:
x_A*y_B = (-6)(9) = -54
x_B*y_A = (2)(7)=14
Difference: -54 -14= -68

B-C:
x_B*y_C=(2)(-3)=-6
x_C*y_B=7*9=63
Difference: -6 -63=-69

C-D:
x_C*y_D=7*0=0
x_D*y_C=(-2)*(-3)=6
Difference: 0 -6 =-6

D-A:
x_D*y_A=(-2)(7)=-14
x_A*y_D=(-6)(0)=0
Difference: -14 -0=-14

-68 + (-69) = -137; then, adding -6 gives -143; plus another -14 is indeed -157.

The area of the quadrilateral with vertices (-6,7), (2,9), (7,-3), and (-2,0) can be found using the shoelace formula.

Step-by-step explanation:

1. List the coordinates in order: A(-6,7), B(2,9), C(7,-3), D(-2,0), back to A(-6,7).

2. Apply the shoelace formula:
   Area = (1/2) * |sum over i (x_i * y_{i+1} - x_{i+1} * y_i)|

3. Compute each term:
   - (-6*9 - 2*7) = -54 -14 = -68
   - (2*(-3) -7*9) = -6 -63 = -69
   - (7*0 - (-2)*(-3)) = 0 -6 = -6
   - ((-2)*7 - (-6)*0) = -14 -0 = -14

4. Sum the terms: -68 + (-69) + (-6) + (-14) = -157

5. Take absolute value and multiply by 1/2: (1/2)*| -157 | = 78.5

Final Answer: "78.5"

To find the equation of a straight line passing through (0,5) and intersecting the x-axis at (-2,0), we can use these two points:

- Point A: (0,5)
- Point B: (-2,0)

The slope \( m \) is calculated as:
\[ m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{0 - 5}{-2 - 0} = \frac{-5}{-2} = \frac{5}{2} \]

Using the point-slope form with Point A:
\[ y - 5 = \frac{5}{2}(x - 0) \]
Simplifying, we get:
\[ y = \frac{5}{2}x + 5 \]

**Answer:**
The equation of the line is \( y = \frac{5}{2}x + 5 \).

So in the required format:

$\boxed{y = \dfrac{5}{2}x + 5}$

Next, I'll calculate the slope between these two points. The formula for slope is m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1). Plugging in the values: m = (-2 - 0)/(-2 - 3) = -2 / -5 = 2/5.

With the slope known, I'll use the point-slope form to write the equation of the line. Using the point (3, 0), the equation becomes y - 0 = (2/5)(x - 3). Simplifying this gives y = (2/5)x - 6/5.

Since substituting x = 8 gives y = 2, which matches the third point's y-coordinate, all three points lie on the same line. Therefore, they are collinear.
</think>

To determine if the three points \((3, 0)\), \((-2, -2)\), and \((8, 2)\) are collinear (lie on the same straight line), we can use the concept of slope.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Calculate the Slope Between Two Points:**
   - Let's first find the slope between points \(A(3, 0)\) and \(B(-2, -2)\).
   \[
   m_{AB} = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{-2 - 0}{-2 - 3} = \frac{-2}{-5} = \frac{2}{5}
   \]

2. **Determine the Equation of the Line Through Points A and B:**
   - Using point-slope form with point \(A(3, 0)\):
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1) \\
   y - 0 = \frac{2}{5}(x - 3) \\
   y = \frac{2}{5}x - \frac{6}{5}
   \]

3. **Check if Point C Lies on This Line:**
   - Now, substitute the coordinates of point \(C(8, 2)\) into the equation:
   \[
   y = \frac{2}{5}x - \frac{6}{5} \\
   2 = \frac{2}{5}(8) - \frac{6}{5} \\
   2 = \frac{16}{5} - \frac{6}{5} \\
   2 = \frac{10}{5} \\
   2 = 2
   \]
   - The equation holds true, so point \(C\) lies on the line defined by points \(A\) and \(B\).

**Conclusion:**
All three points lie on the same straight line. Therefore, they are collinear.

Okay, so I have this problem where I need to find the equation of a line that makes an angle of 2π/3 with the positive x-axis and passes through the point (0, 2). Then, I also need to find the equation of another line that's parallel to this one and intersects the negative y-axis at a distance of 2 units from the origin.

First, I should figure out the slope of the first line. The angle given is 120 degrees because 2π/3 radians equals 120 degrees in terms of pi being 180. Since it's measured from the positive x-axis, the tangent of this angle will give me the slope.

So, tan(120°) is equal to tan(180° - 60°), which is -tan(60°). I know that tan(60°) is √3, so tan(120°) should be -√3. Therefore, the slope (m) of the first line is -√3.

Now, using the point-slope form of a line's equation: y - y₁ = m(x - x₁), where (x₁, y₁) is the point it passes through, which in this case is (0, 2). Plugging these values into the formula:

y - 2 = -√3(x - 0)

Simplifying that gives me:

y = -√3 x + 2

So, the equation of the first line is y = -√3 x + 2.

Next, I need to find a line parallel to this one. Parallel lines have the same slope, so the second line will also have a slope of -√3. Additionally, it needs to intersect the negative y-axis at a point that's 2 units away from the origin. 

The negative y-axis is where x = 0 and y is negative. So being 2 units away would mean either (0, -2) or maybe considering direction? Wait, but if it's on the negative y-axis and 2 units from the origin, it must be at (0, -2). 

So using the point-slope form again for this second line, passing through (0, -2):

y - (-2) = -√3(x - 0)
Simplifying:

y + 2 = -√3 x
Which gives:
y = -√3 x - 2

I think that's it. The two lines are parallel with slopes of -√3 and intercepts at (0, 2) and (0, -2), respectively.
</think>

The equation of the line is y = -√3 x + 2, and the parallel line intersecting the negative y-axis is y = -√3 x - 2.

Step-by-step explanation:

1. **Determine the slope using the given angle:**
   The angle with the positive x-axis is 2π/3 radians (or 120 degrees). The slope m is tan(120°) which equals -√3.

2. **Find the equation of the first line passing through (0, 2):**
   Using point-slope form: y = mx + b.
   Substituting m = -√3 and the point (0, 2):
   y = -√3 x + 2

3. **Determine the second parallel line intercepting negative y-axis at distance 2 from origin:**
   Since it's parallel, slope is also -√3. It intersects y-axis at (0, -2).
   Using point-slope form:
   y = -√3 x - 2

**Final Answer:**
The equations of the lines are {y = -√3 x + 2} and {y = -√3 x - 2}.

Now, let's provide the step-by-step explanation:

We are given the point (4, 4) lying on a line with x-intercept 'a' and y-intercept 'b'. The equation of such a line can be written in intercept form as:
\[ \frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1 \]

Since the point (4, 4) lies on this line, substituting gives:
\[ \frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{b} = 1 \quad \text{(Equation 1)} \]

We are also given that \( |a| + |b| = k \). Without loss of generality, assume a is positive (since if a were negative, the analysis would follow similarly but with appropriate sign considerations).

Let’s consider two cases:

**Case 1:** Both intercepts are positive.
- So, Equation 1 becomes:
\[ \frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{b} = 1 \]
And we have \( a + b = k \).
Expressing from the sum:
\[ b = k - a \]
Substitute into Equation 1:
\[ \frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{k - a} = 1 \]
Multiply through by \( a(k - a) \):
\[ 4(k - a) + 4a = a(k - a) \]
Simplify:
\[ 4k - 4a + 4a = ak - a^2 \]
\[ 4k = ak - a^2 \]
Rearranged to quadratic form:
\[ a^2 - (k + 4)a + 4k = 0 \]

Compute discriminant \( D \):
\[ D = [-(k + 4)]^2 - 4(1)(4k) = k^2 + 8k + 16 - 16k = k^2 - 8k + 16 = (k - 4)^2 \]
Since \( D \geq 0 \), real roots exist for any \( k \). However, substituting specific values shows that when solving for a particular point and intercepts sum condition, the discriminant must be non-negative. For our problem, testing with k=13:

Substitute k=13 into quadratic:
\[ a^2 - 17a + 52 = 0 \]
Compute discriminant:
\[ D = (-17)^2 -4(1)(52) = 289 - 208 = 81 > 0 \]
Thus, real roots exist. Solving gives a valid positive intercept, hence k=13 is possible.

**Case 2:** One intercept is negative.
Assume 'a' is positive and 'b' is negative (similar reasoning applies if vice versa).

Equation 1:
\[ \frac{4}{a} - \frac{4}{|b|} = 1 \]
Let \( |b| = c \), so Equation becomes:
\[ \frac{4}{a} - \frac{4}{c} = 1 \quad \text{(Equation 2)} \]
And sum of intercepts (since b is negative):
\[ a + c = k \]

From Equation 2, express \( c = \frac{4(a - 1)}{a(1)} \) but proper substitution leads to:
Rearranging Equation 2:
\[ \frac{4}{a} - \frac{4}{c} = 1 \]
Multiply through by \( a c \):
\[ 4c - 4a = a c \]
Express from sum equation: \( c = k - a \)
Substitute into above equation:
\[ 4(k - a) - 4a = a(k - a) \]
Simplify:
\[ 4k - 8a = a k - a^2 \]
Rearranged to quadratic form:
\[ a^2 - (k + 8)a + 4k = 0 \]

Compute discriminant D:
Same as before, \( D = (- (k+8))^2 -16k = k^2 +16k +64 -16k = k^2 +64 >0 \) for all real k.

Thus, quadratic yields real solutions. For our problem with k=13:
Quadratic equation becomes:
\[ a^2 - 21a +52=0 \]
Compute discriminant:
\( D= (21)^2-4*1*52 = 441 - 208=233 >0 \)
Thus, real roots exist. Solving yields valid intercepts where a is positive and c=k-a>0.

**Conclusion:**
Both cases yield valid solutions for k=13, confirming that it's possible to have such lines passing through (4,4) with the sum of intercepts equal to 13.

\boxed{13}

d^2 = [x_N - x_M]^2 + [y_N - y_M]^2

5m(3 +m)=15m +5m²

17m(1 -3m)=17m -51m²

To find the distance \( d \) between points \( M \) and \( N \), where line \( l \) passes through \( (-4, 0) \) and intersects lines \( y = x \) at \( M \) and \( y = 3x \) at \( N \):

1. **Equation of Line \( l \):** Assume the slope is \( m \). The equation is \( y = m(x + 4) \).

2. **Find Coordinates of \( M \):**
   - Intersection with \( y = x \):
     \[
     m(x + 4) = x \implies (m - 1)x = -4x \implies t = \frac{4}{m - 1}
     \]
   - Substituting back:
     \[
     M\left(\frac{-4(m + 1)}{m - 1}, \frac{4m}{m - 1}\right)
     \]

3. **Find Coordinates of \( N \):**
   - Intersection with \( y = 3x \):
     \[
     m(x + 4) = 3x \implies (3 - m)x = 4m \implies x = \frac{4m}{3 - m}
     \]
   - Substituting back:
     \[
     N\left(\frac{12 - 8m}{(3 - m)^2}, \frac{12m}{(3 - m)}\right)
     \]

4. **Calculate Distance \( d \):**
   \[
   d = \sqrt{\left(\frac{-8(m + 1)}{(3 - m)(m - 1)}\right)^2 + \left(\frac{8m^2}{(3 - m)(m - 1)}\right)^2}
   \]
   Simplifying:
   \[
   d = \frac{8|m|\sqrt{m^2 + 1}}{|(3 - m)(m - 1)|}
   \]

Thus, the distance \( MN \) is:

**Final Answer:**
\boxed{\dfrac{8|m|\sqrt{m^{2}+1}}{|(m-1)(m-3)|}}

To find the distance from the point (5, -7) to the line 5x - 6y - 28 = 0,

I'll use the formula for the distance from a point (x₀, y₀) to the line ax + by + c = 0:

Distance = |a*x₀ + b*y₀ + c| / sqrt(a² + b²)

First, I identify the coefficients:
a = 5
b = -6
c = -28

Next, substitute the point (5, -7) into the formula:
Numerator = |5*5 + (-6)*(-7) + (-28)| = |25 + 42 - 28| = |39|

Denominator = sqrt(5² + (-6)²) = sqrt(25 + 36) = sqrt(61)

Finally, calculate the distance:
Distance = 39 / sqrt(61)

The formula for calculating the distance \( d \) between two parallel lines given by the equations \( Ax + By + C_1 = 0 \) and \( Ax + By + C_2 = 0 \) is:

\[
d = \frac{|C_1 - C_2|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

For the given lines:
- First line: \( 3x - 4y + 12 = 0 \) (where \( A = 3 \), \( B = -4 \), \( C_1 = 12 \))
- Second line: \( 3x - 4y + 7 = 0 \) (where \( A = 3 \), \( B = -4 \), \( C_2 = 7 \))

Substituting these values into the formula:

\[
d = \frac{|12 - 7|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2}} = \frac{5}{\sqrt{9 + 16}} = \frac{5}{\sqrt{25}} = \frac{5}{5} = 1
\]

The distance between the two parallel lines is **1** unit.

To find the distance from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(y = mx + c\), you can use the formula:

\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|mx_0 - y_0 + c|}{\sqrt{m^2 + 1}}
\]

For your point \((2, 4)\), plug in \(x_0 = 2\) and \(y_0 = 4\):

\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|2m - 4 + c|}{\sqrt{m^2 + 1}}
\]

The coordinates of the foot of the perpendicular from the point (1, 5) to the line 3x - 4y - 16 = 0 can be found using the formula for projecting a point onto a line. This involves solving the system of equations derived from the given line and its perpendicular counterpart.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Find the Slope of the Given Line:**
   The equation is 3x - 4y - 16 = 0.
   Convert to slope-intercept form:
   \[
   -4y = -3x + 16 \implies y = \frac{3}{4}x - 4
   \]
   So, the slope \( m \) is \( \frac{3}{4} \).

2. **Determine the Slope of the Perpendicular Line:**
   The slope of a line perpendicular to this is the negative reciprocal:
   \[
   m_{\text{perp}} = -\frac{4}{3}
   \]

3. **Equation of the Perpendicular Line Through (1, 5):**
   Using point-slope form:
   \[
   y - 5 = -\frac{4}{3}(x - 1)
   \]
   Multiply through by 3 to eliminate fractions and rearrange:
   \[
   4x + 3y = 19
   \]

4. **Solve the System of Equations:**
   The system is:
   \[
   \begin{cases}
   3x - 4y = 16 \\
   4x + 3y = 19
   \end{cases}
   \]
   Multiply the first equation by 3 and the second by 4 to eliminate \( y \):
   \[
   9x - 12y = 48 \\
   16x + 12y = 76
   \]
   Add both equations:
   \[
   25x = 124 \implies x = \frac{124}{25}
   \]
   Substitute \( x \) back into one of the original equations to find \( y \):
   \[
   3\left(\frac{124}{25}\right) - 4y = 16 \\
   \frac{372}{25} - 4y = 16 \\
   -4y = 16 - \frac{372}{25} \\
   y = -\frac{7}{25}
   \]

**Final Answer:**
The foot of the perpendicular is at \(\left( \frac{124}{25}, -\frac{7}{25} \right)\).

The equation of the perpendicular bisector of the line segment joining the points (5,6) and (1,4) can be found by first determining the midpoint and then finding the negative reciprocal of the original slope. 

1. **Midpoint Calculation**:
   The midpoint M between two points (x₁,y₁) and (x₂,y₂) is given by M = ((x₁+x₂)/2, (y₁+y₂)/2).
   For the points (5,6) and (1,4):
   - Midpoint M_x = (5 + 1)/2 = 3
   - Midpoint M_y = (6 + 4)/2 = 5
   So, the midpoint is (3,5).

2. **Slope of Original Line**:
   The slope m between two points is given by m = (y₂ - y₁)/(x₂ - x₁).
   For the points (5,6) and (1,4):
   - Slope m = (4 - 6)/(1 - 5) = (-2)/(-4) = 0.5

3. **Slope of Perpendicular Bisector**:
   The slope of a line perpendicular to another is the negative reciprocal of its slope.
   - Perpendicular slope = -1/0.5 = -2

4. **Equation of Perpendicular Bisector**:
   Using the point-slope form: y - y₁ = m(x - x₁), where (x₁,y₁) is the midpoint (3,5):
   - y - 5 = -2(x - 3)
   Simplifying:
     - y - 5 = -2x + 6
     - y = -2x + 11

So, the equation of the perpendicular bisector is:

Point (5, -9):

Denominator:A² + B²=49+81=130

So x =5 -7*(97)/130 =5 -679/130= (650 -679)/130= (-29)/130 ≈-0.223

y=-9 -(-9)*(97)/130= -9 + 873/130= (-1170 +873)/130=(-297)/130≈-2.285

So the foot is at ( -29/130, -297/130 ). Simplify fractions? Maybe leave as they are.

First, I need to find the equation of a line perpendicular to the given line X - 7y + 5 = 0. The slope of the original line is determined by rearranging it into slope-intercept form (Y = mx + b), where m represents the slope.

Once I have the slope of the original line, the slope of the perpendicular line will be the negative reciprocal of that value.

With the new slope known and a specific point through which the perpendicular line passes (in this case, when X = 5, Y is found by substituting into the original equation), I can use the point-slope form to determine the complete equation of the desired perpendicular line.
</think>

To find the equation of the line that is **perpendicular** to the given line \( X - 7y + 5 = 0 \) and passes through the point where \( X = 5 \), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Find the Slope of the Given Line
First, rewrite the given equation in slope-intercept form (\( Y = mX + b \)) to identify its slope.

Starting with:
\[ X - 7y + 5 = 0 \]

Rearrange to solve for \( Y \):
\[
\begin{align*}
-7y &= -X - 5 \\
y &= \frac{1}{7}X + \frac{5}{7}
\end{align*}
\]
The slope (\( m_{\text{given}} \)) of the given line is \( \frac{1}{7} \).

### Step 2: Determine the Slope of the Perpendicular Line
The slope of a line perpendicular to another is the negative reciprocal of the original slope.

So, the slope (\( m_{\perp} \)) of the perpendicular line is:
\[ m_{\perp} = -7 \]

### Step 3: Identify a Point on the Given Line
We know that when \( X = 5 \), we can find the corresponding \( Y \) value by substituting into the original equation.

Substitute \( X = 5 \):
\[
\begin{align*}
5 - 7y + 5 &= 0 \\
10 - 7y &= 0 \\
-7y &= -10 \\
y &= \frac{10}{7}
\end{align*}
\]
Thus, the point is \( \left(5, \frac{10}{7}\right) \).

### Step 4: Use the Point-Slope Form to Find the Equation
Using the slope of the perpendicular line (\( m = -7 \)) and the point \( \left(5, \frac{10}{7}\right) \), apply the point-slope formula:
\[
y - y_1 = m(X - X_1)
\]
Substitute the known values:
\[
y - \frac{10}{7} = -7(X - 5)
\]

### Step 5: Simplify to Standard Form
Expand and simplify the equation:
\[
\begin{align*}
y - \frac{10}{7} &= -7X + 35 \\
y &= -7X + 35 + \frac{10}{7} \\
y &= -7X + \frac{245}{7} + \frac{10}{7} \\
y &= -7X + \frac{255}{7}
\end{align*}
\]
Multiply every term by 7 to eliminate the fraction:
\[
7y = -49X + 255
\]
Rearrange to standard form (\( AX + By + C = 0 \)):
\[
49X + 7y - 255 = 0
\]

### Final Answer
The equation of the perpendicular line is:
\[
\boxed{49X + 7y - 255 = 0}
\]

To find the equation of a line that is **parallel** to the given line \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) and passes through the point \((-4, 5)\), follow these steps:

1. **Understand Parallel Lines**:
   - **Parallel lines** have the same slope.
   - Therefore, any line parallel to \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) will have the same coefficients for \(x\) and \(y\).

2. **Formulate the General Equation of the Parallel Line**:
   \[
   5x - 6y + c = 0
   \]
   where \(c\) is a constant to be determined.

3. **Substitute the Given Point into the Equation**:
   - Plug in \(x = -4\) and \(y = 5\):
     \[
     5(-4) - 6(5) + c = 0
     \]
   
4. **Solve for \(c\)**:
   \[
   -20 - 30 + c = 0 \\
   -50 + c = 0 \\
   c = 50
   \]

5. **Write the Final Equation of the Parallel Line**:
   \[
   5x - 6y + 50 = 0
   \]

**Final Answer:**

\boxed{5x - 6y + 50 = 0}

1. Expand both sides of the equation: 
   - Left side: 24(x + 12) = 24x + 288
   - Right side: 10(y - 4) = 10y - 40

2. Rearrange to standard form:
   24x + 288 = 10y - 40 → 24x - 10y + 328 = 0
   So, A = 24, B = -10, C = 328

3. Plug the point (-2, 2) into the distance formula:
   |(24*(-2)) + (-10*2) + 328| / sqrt(24² + (-10)^2)
   Numerator: |-48 - 20 + 328| = |260|
   Denominator: sqrt(576 + 100) = sqrt(676) = 26

4. Compute the distance:
   260 / 26 = 10

Final Answer: "Numeric answer": 10

To find the distance from point \( P(4,1) \) to the line \( 4x - y = 0 \), we can use the formula for the distance from a point to a line:

\[
d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Here, the line equation is \( 4x - y = 0 \), which can be written as \( 4x - y + 0 = 0 \). So, \( A = 4 \), \( B = -1 \), and \( C = 0 \).

Substituting the values into the formula:

\[
d = \frac{|4 \cdot 4 + (-1) \cdot 1 + 0|}{\sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2}} = \frac{|16 - 1|}{\sqrt{16 + 1}} = \frac{15}{\sqrt{17}}
\]

The final answer is:

\[
\boxed{\dfrac{15}{\sqrt{17}}}
\]

Sure! Let's solve the problem step by step.

**Given:**
- Two points on a line: (0, 8) and (6, 10).
- Another point: (10, 14) and an unknown point (এক্স, 26).

We need to find the value of "�ক্স" such that the line passing through (10, 14) and (�ক্স, 26) is **perpendicular** to the line passing through (0, 8) and (6, 10).

---

### Step 1: Find the Slope of the First Line

The slope (\(m_1\)) of a line passing through two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is calculated as:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

For the points (0, 8) and (6, 10):
\[
m_1 = \frac{10 - 8}{6 - 0} = \frac{2}{6} = \frac{1}{3}
\]

---

### Step 2: Determine the Slope of the Perpendicular Line

Two lines are perpendicular if the product of their slopes is \(-1\). Therefore, the slope (\(m_2\)) of the line perpendicular to the first line is:
\[
m_1 \times m_2 = -1 \\
\frac{1}{3} \times m_2 = -1 \\
m_2 = -3
\]

---

### Step 3: Use Point-Slope Form to Find the Equation of the Perpendicular Line

Using one of the points (e.g., (10, 14)) and the slope \(m_2 = -3\), we can write the equation of the line in **point-slope form**:
\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]

Substituting the values:
\[
y - 14 = -3(x - 10)
\]

Expanding and simplifying:
\[
y - 14 = -3x + 30 \\
y = -3x + 44
\]

---

### Step 4: Find the Value of "�ক্স"

We know that the line passes through the point (�ক্স, 26). Substitute \(y = 26\) into the equation:
\[
26 = -3x + 44 \\
-3x = 26 - 44 \\
-3x = -18 \\
x = \frac{-18}{-3} \\
x = 6
\]

---

**Final Answer:**

\boxed{6}

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **List the Coordinates:** 
   Arrange the vertices in order (A(-6,7), B(2,9), C(7,-3), D(-2,0)) and repeat the first vertex at the end to close the polygon.

2. **Apply Shoelace Formula:**
   - Multiply each x-coordinate by the next y-coordinate and sum them up.
     Sum1 = (-6)*9 + 2*(-3) +7*0 + (-2)*7 = -54 -6 + 0 -14 = -74
   - Multiply each y-coordinate by the next x-coordinate and sum them up.
     Sum2 = 7*2 +9*7 + (-3)*(-2)+0*(-6) =14+63+6+0=83

3. **Calculate Area:**
   |Sum2 - Sum1| / 2 = |83 - (-74)|/2 = 157/2 = 78.5.

**Answer:** The area of the quadrilateral is \boxed{78.5} square units.

To determine a point on the x-axis equidistant from (9,8) and (5,6):

1. **Find the perpendicular bisector** between the two points:
   - Midpoint: ((9+5)/2, (8+6)/2) = (7, 7)
   - Slope of line segment: m = (6-8)/(5-9) = (-2)/(-4) = 1/2
   - Perpendicular slope: -2
   - Equation: y - 7 = -2(x - 7)

2. **Find intersection with x-axis** by setting y=0:

Thus, the point is (10.5, 0).

The slope (m) of a line passing through two points \( P(x_1, y_1) \) and \( Q(x_2, y_2) \) is calculated using the formula:

\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

Given:
- Point P has coordinates (2, -2), so \( x_1 = 2 \) and \( y_1 = -2 \).
- Point Q has coordinates (10, 2), so \( x_2 = 10 \) and \( y_2 = 2 \).

Plugging in the values:

\[
m = \frac{2 - (-2)}{10 - 2} = \frac{4}{8} = 0.5
\]

Final Answer: {"Numeric answer": "0.5"}

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the First Line:**
   - The first line lies along the x-axis. Its equation is y = 0, so its slope (m₁) is 0.
   
2. **Calculate Slope of the Second Line:**
   - Points given are (5, 1) and (6, 0).
   - Slope formula: m = (y₂ - y₁)/(x₂ - x₁)
   - Plugging in values:
     m = (0 - 1)/(6 - 5) = (-1)/1 = -1
   - So, the slope of the second line is -1.

3. **Find Angles with Respect to X-Axis:**
   - For the first line (slope 0), angle θ₁ = arctan(0) = 0°.
   - For the second line (slope -1), calculate θ₂:
     tanθ₂ = |-1| → θ₂ = 45° below the x-axis, but since we measure angles from the positive x-axis, it's equivalent to a 45° angle in the negative direction.

4. **Determine the Angle Between the Lines:**
   - The lines form an angle equal to the difference between their respective angles relative to the x-axis.
   - Since one is at 0° and the other effectively at 135° (since it's sloping downward), but we consider the smallest positive angle between them:
     Angle = |135° - 0°| = 45°

First, I need to find the equation of a line that passes through the points (3, 1) and (5, 7). To do this, I'll start by calculating the slope (m) using the formula:

y - y₁ = m(x - x₁)

</think>

Sure! Let's find the equation of the line that passes through the points (3, 1) and (5, 7).

### Step 1: Calculate the Slope (m)
The formula for the slope between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

Plugging in the given points:
\[
m = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3
\]

### Step 2: Use the Point-Slope Form to Find the Equation
The point-slope form of a linear equation is:
\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]

Using the point (3, 1):
\[
y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
\]

### Step 3: Simplify to Slope-Intercept Form
Simplify the equation:
\[
y - 1 = 3x - 9 \\
y = 3x - 8
\]

### Final Answer
The equation of the line in slope-intercept form is:

\[
\boxed{y = 3x - 8}
\]

The linear relationship between price (P) and quantity sold (Q) can be determined using two data points: (114, 1080) and (116, 1320). 

1. **Calculate the slope (a):**
   \[
   a = \frac{1320 - 1080}{116 - 114} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
   \]

2. **Determine the y-intercept (b) using one of the points:**
   Using P=114 and Q=1080:
   \[
   1080 = 120(114) + b \\
   1080 = 13680 + b \\
   b = 1080 - 13680 = -12600
   \]

3. **Equation of the line:**
   \[
   Q = 120P - 12600
   \]

4. **Calculate Q when P=117:**
   \[
   Q = 120(117) - 12600 \\
   Q = 14040 - 12600 \\
   Q = 1440
   \]

**Answer:** At a price of 117 per liter, the vendor can expect to sell **1,440 liters**.

The equation of the line joining points (1,0) and (2,3) is found using the slope formula: m = 3. Using point-slope form gives y = 3x - 3.

**Step-by-step Explanation:**

1. **Calculate the Slope (m):**
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{3 - 0}{2 - 1} = 3
   \]

2. **Use Point-Slope Form:**
   Taking point (1, 0):
   \[
   y - 0 = 3(x - 1)
   \]
   
3. **Simplify to Slope-Intercept Form:**
   \[
   y = 3x - 3
   \]

**Answer:** The equation of the line is \( y = 3x - 3 \).

To find the equation of the line passing through the points (4, 7) and (-5, 8), follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the slope (m):**
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{8 - 7}{-5 - 4} = \frac{1}{-9} = -\frac{1}{9}
   \]

2. **Use the point-slope form with one of the points (e.g., (4, 7)):**
   \[
   y - 7 = -\frac{1}{9}(x - 4)
   \]

3. **Convert to slope-intercept form (y = mx + b):**
   \[
   y = -\frac{1}{9}x + \frac{4}{9} + 7
   \]
   Convert 7 to ninths: \(7 = \frac{63}{9}\)
   \[
   y = -\frac{1}{9}x + \frac{67}{9}
   \]

**Final Answer:**  
The equation of the line is **\(y = -\frac{1}{9}x + \frac{67}{9}\)**.

The distance from the point (5, -7) to the line 5x - 6y -28 = 0 can be found using the formula for the distance from a point to a line. 

Step-by-step explanation:

1. **Identify coefficients**: 
   The given line is in the form Ax + By + C = 0, where A=5, B=-6, and C=-28.

2. **Apply the distance formula**:
   d = |A*x0 + B*y0 + C| / sqrt(A² + B²)

3. **Plug in the values**:
   x0 = 5, y0 = -7
   A*5 = 25
   B*(-7) = (-6)*(-7) = 42
   So, numerator becomes |25 + 42 - 28| = |39|

4. **Calculate the denominator**:
   sqrt(5² + (-6)²) = sqrt(25 + 36) = sqrt(61)

5. **Final distance calculation**:
   d = 39 / sqrt(61), which can also be rationalized as (39*sqrt(61))/61.

Answer: {Final Answer: "39/sqrt(61)"}

So plugging in the values: (4 - 1)/(3 - 6) = 3/(-3) = -1. So the slope of BC is -1.

Since the altitude from A is perpendicular to BC, its slope will be the negative reciprocal of -1. The negative reciprocal of a number is found by flipping the numerator and denominator and changing the sign. So the reciprocal of -1 is -1/1, but since we need the negative of that, it becomes 1.

Next, I'll substitute the coordinates of the point \((-4, 5)\) into this equation to find the value of \(c\).

To find the equation of a line that is **parallel** to the given line \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) and passes through the point \((-4, 5)\), follow these steps:

1. **Understand Parallel Lines**:  
   Two lines are parallel if they have the same slope. The general form of a line is \(Ax + By + C = 0\). Therefore, any line parallel to \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) will have coefficients for \(x\) and \(y\) that are proportional to these values.

2. **Set Up the Equation**:  
   The equation of a line parallel to \(5x - 6y + 4 = 0\) can be written as:  
   \[
   5x - 6y + C = 0
   \]  
   where \(C\) is a constant that needs to be determined.

3. **Substitute the Given Point**:  
   The line passes through the point \((-4, 5)\). Substitute \(x = -4\) and \(y = 5\) into the equation:  
   \[
   5(-4) - 6(5) + C = 0
   \]

4. **Calculate the Constant \(C\)**:  
   Simplify the equation:  
   \[
   -20 - 30 + C = 0 \\
   -50 + C = 0 \\
   C = 50
   \]

5. **Write the Final Equation**:  
   Replace \(C\) in the equation to get the final form of the line:  
   \[
   5x - 6y + 50 = 0
   \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{5x - 6y + 50 = 0}
\]

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Simplify the Given Line Equation:**
   
   Start with the equation:
   \[
   24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)
   \]
   
   Expand both sides:
   \[
   24x + 288 = 10y - 40
   \]
   
   Rearrange to standard form \(Ax + By + C = 0\):
   \[
   24x - 10y + 328 = 0
   \]

2. **Identify Coefficients:**
   
   From the equation \(24x - 10y + 328 = 0\), we have:
   \[
   A = 24,\quad B = -10,\quad C = 328
   \]

3. **Use Distance Formula:**
   
   The distance (\(d\)) from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is given by:
   \[
   d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
   \]
   
4. **Plug in Values:**
   
   Given point \((-2, 2)\):
   \[
   d = \frac{|24(-2) + (-10)(2) + 328|}{\sqrt{24^2 + (-10)^2}}
   \]
   
5. **Calculate Numerator:**
   
   Compute the numerator:
   \[
   | -48 - 20 + 328 | = |260| = 260
   \]

6. **Calculate Denominator:**
   
   Compute the denominator:
   \[
   \sqrt{576 + 100} = \sqrt{676} = 26
   \]

7. **Find Distance:**
   
   Divide numerator by denominator:
   \[
   d = \frac{260}{26} = 10
   \]
   
   However, there seems to be a miscalculation in the intermediate steps provided earlier. Upon re-evaluating with correct calculations:

8. **Correct Final Calculation:**
   
   Let's recalculate:
   
   From step 5 and 6:
   \[
   d = \frac{260}{\sqrt{676}} = \frac{260}{26} = 10
   \]
   
   So, the correct distance is **10 units**.

9. **Reconciliation with Earlier Answer:**
   
   It appears there was an inconsistency in the initial step-by-step explanation where the final answer was incorrectly stated as 32 units instead of 10 units. The accurate distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line is **10 units**.

The distance from the point P(4,1) to the line 4x - y = 0 can be calculated using the formula:

Distance \( d = \frac{|Ax + By + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}} \)

Here, A = 4, B = -1, C = 0. Plugging in the values:

\( d = \frac{|4*4 + (-1)*1 + 0|}{\sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2}} = \frac{15}{\sqrt{17}} = \frac{15\sqrt{17}}{17} \)

**Final Answer:**  
The distance is \( \frac{15\sqrt{17}}{17} \).
